```kotlin
// Async support

// Public interface members begin here.


// Interface implemented by anything that can contain an object reference.
//
// Such types expose a `destroy()` method that must be called to cleanly
// dispose of the contained objects. Failure to call this method may result
// in memory leaks.
//
// The easiest way to ensure this method is called is to use the `.use`
// helper method to execute a block and destroy the object at the end.
interface Disposable {
    fun destroy()
    companion object {
        fun destroy(vararg args: Any?) {
            args.filterIsInstance<Disposable>()
                .forEach(Disposable::destroy)
        }
    }
}

/**
 * @suppress
 */
inline fun <T : Disposable?, R> T.use(block: (T) -> R) =
    try {
        block(this)
    } finally {
        try {
            // N.B. our implementation is on the nullable type `Disposable?`.
            this?.destroy()
        } catch (e: Throwable) {
            // swallow
        }
    }

/** 
 * Used to instantiate an interface without an actual pointer, for fakes in tests, mostly.
 *
 * @suppress
 * */
object NoPointer

/**
 * @suppress
 */
public object FfiConverterUInt: FfiConverter<UInt, Int> {
    override fun lift(value: Int): UInt {
        return value.toUInt()
    }

    override fun read(buf: ByteBuffer): UInt {
        return lift(buf.getInt())
    }

    override fun lower(value: UInt): Int {
        return value.toInt()
    }

    override fun allocationSize(value: UInt) = 4UL

    override fun write(value: UInt, buf: ByteBuffer) {
        buf.putInt(value.toInt())
    }
}

/**
 * @suppress
 */
public object FfiConverterULong: FfiConverter<ULong, Long> {
    override fun lift(value: Long): ULong {
        return value.toULong()
    }

    override fun read(buf: ByteBuffer): ULong {
        return lift(buf.getLong())
    }

    override fun lower(value: ULong): Long {
        return value.toLong()
    }

    override fun allocationSize(value: ULong) = 8UL

    override fun write(value: ULong, buf: ByteBuffer) {
        buf.putLong(value.toLong())
    }
}

/**
 * @suppress
 */
public object FfiConverterBoolean: FfiConverter<Boolean, Byte> {
    override fun lift(value: Byte): Boolean {
        return value.toInt() != 0
    }

    override fun read(buf: ByteBuffer): Boolean {
        return lift(buf.get())
    }

    override fun lower(value: Boolean): Byte {
        return if (value) 1.toByte() else 0.toByte()
    }

    override fun allocationSize(value: Boolean) = 1UL

    override fun write(value: Boolean, buf: ByteBuffer) {
        buf.put(lower(value))
    }
}

/**
 * @suppress
 */
public object FfiConverterString: FfiConverter<String, RustBuffer.ByValue> {
    // Note: we don't inherit from FfiConverterRustBuffer, because we use a
    // special encoding when lowering/lifting.  We can use `RustBuffer.len` to
    // store our length and avoid writing it out to the buffer.
    override fun lift(value: RustBuffer.ByValue): String {
        try {
            val byteArr = ByteArray(value.len.toInt())
            value.asByteBuffer()!!.get(byteArr)
            return byteArr.toString(Charsets.UTF_8)
        } finally {
            RustBuffer.free(value)
        }
    }

    override fun read(buf: ByteBuffer): String {
        val len = buf.getInt()
        val byteArr = ByteArray(len)
        buf.get(byteArr)
        return byteArr.toString(Charsets.UTF_8)
    }

    fun toUtf8(value: String): ByteBuffer {
        // Make sure we don't have invalid UTF-16, check for lone surrogates.
        return Charsets.UTF_8.newEncoder().run {
            onMalformedInput(CodingErrorAction.REPORT)
            encode(CharBuffer.wrap(value))
        }
    }

    override fun lower(value: String): RustBuffer.ByValue {
        val byteBuf = toUtf8(value)
        // Ideally we'd pass these bytes to `ffi_bytebuffer_from_bytes`, but doing so would require us
        // to copy them into a JNA `Memory`. So we might as well directly copy them into a `RustBuffer`.
        val rbuf = RustBuffer.alloc(byteBuf.limit().toULong())
        rbuf.asByteBuffer()!!.put(byteBuf)
        return rbuf
    }

    // We aren't sure exactly how many bytes our string will be once it's UTF-8
    // encoded.  Allocate 3 bytes per UTF-16 code unit which will always be
    // enough.
    override fun allocationSize(value: String): ULong {
        val sizeForLength = 4UL
        val sizeForString = value.length.toULong() * 3UL
        return sizeForLength + sizeForString
    }

    override fun write(value: String, buf: ByteBuffer) {
        val byteBuf = toUtf8(value)
        buf.putInt(byteBuf.limit())
        buf.put(byteBuf)
    }
}

/**
 * @suppress
 */
public object FfiConverterByteArray: FfiConverterRustBuffer<ByteArray> {
    override fun read(buf: ByteBuffer): ByteArray {
        val len = buf.getInt()
        val byteArr = ByteArray(len)
        buf.get(byteArr)
        return byteArr
    }
    override fun allocationSize(value: ByteArray): ULong {
        return 4UL + value.size.toULong()
    }
    override fun write(value: ByteArray, buf: ByteBuffer) {
        buf.putInt(value.size)
        buf.put(value)
    }
}


// This template implements a class for working with a Rust struct via a Pointer/Arc<T>
// to the live Rust struct on the other side of the FFI.
//
// Each instance implements core operations for working with the Rust `Arc<T>` and the
// Kotlin Pointer to work with the live Rust struct on the other side of the FFI.
//
// There's some subtlety here, because we have to be careful not to operate on a Rust
// struct after it has been dropped, and because we must expose a public API for freeing
// theq Kotlin wrapper object in lieu of reliable finalizers. The core requirements are:
//
//   * Each instance holds an opaque pointer to the underlying Rust struct.
//     Method calls need to read this pointer from the object's state and pass it in to
//     the Rust FFI.
//
//   * When an instance is no longer needed, its pointer should be passed to a
//     special destructor function provided by the Rust FFI, which will drop the
//     underlying Rust struct.
//
//   * Given an instance, calling code is expected to call the special
//     `destroy` method in order to free it after use, either by calling it explicitly
//     or by using a higher-level helper like the `use` method. Failing to do so risks
//     leaking the underlying Rust struct.
//
//   * We can't assume that calling code will do the right thing, and must be prepared
//     to handle Kotlin method calls executing concurrently with or even after a call to
//     `destroy`, and to handle multiple (possibly concurrent!) calls to `destroy`.
//
//   * We must never allow Rust code to operate on the underlying Rust struct after
//     the destructor has been called, and must never call the destructor more than once.
//     Doing so may trigger memory unsafety.
//
//   * To mitigate many of the risks of leaking memory and use-after-free unsafety, a `Cleaner`
//     is implemented to call the destructor when the Kotlin object becomes unreachable.
//     This is done in a background thread. This is not a panacea, and client code should be aware that
//      1. the thread may starve if some there are objects that have poorly performing
//     `drop` methods or do significant work in their `drop` methods.
//      2. the thread is shared across the whole library. This can be tuned by using `android_cleaner = true`,
//         or `android = true` in the [`kotlin` section of the `uniffi.toml` file](https://mozilla.github.io/uniffi-rs/kotlin/configuration.html).
//
// If we try to implement this with mutual exclusion on access to the pointer, there is the
// possibility of a race between a method call and a concurrent call to `destroy`:
//
//    * Thread A starts a method call, reads the value of the pointer, but is interrupted
//      before it can pass the pointer over the FFI to Rust.
//    * Thread B calls `destroy` and frees the underlying Rust struct.
//    * Thread A resumes, passing the already-read pointer value to Rust and triggering
//      a use-after-free.
//
// One possible solution would be to use a `ReadWriteLock`, with each method call taking
// a read lock (and thus allowed to run concurrently) and the special `destroy` method
// taking a write lock (and thus blocking on live method calls). However, we aim not to
// generate methods with any hidden blocking semantics, and a `destroy` method that might
// block if called incorrectly seems to meet that bar.
//
// So, we achieve our goals by giving each instance an associated `AtomicLong` counter to track
// the number of in-flight method calls, and an `AtomicBoolean` flag to indicate whether `destroy`
// has been called. These are updated according to the following rules:
//
//    * The initial value of the counter is 1, indicating a live object with no in-flight calls.
//      The initial value for the flag is false.
//
//    * At the start of each method call, we atomically check the counter.
//      If it is 0 then the underlying Rust struct has already been destroyed and the call is aborted.
//      If it is nonzero them we atomically increment it by 1 and proceed with the method call.
//
//    * At the end of each method call, we atomically decrement and check the counter.
//      If it has reached zero then we destroy the underlying Rust struct.
//
//    * When `destroy` is called, we atomically flip the flag from false to true.
//      If the flag was already true we silently fail.
//      Otherwise we atomically decrement and check the counter.
//      If it has reached zero then we destroy the underlying Rust struct.
//
// Astute readers may observe that this all sounds very similar to the way that Rust's `Arc<T>` works,
// and indeed it is, with the addition of a flag to guard against multiple calls to `destroy`.
//
// The overall effect is that the underlying Rust struct is destroyed only when `destroy` has been
// called *and* all in-flight method calls have completed, avoiding violating any of the expectations
// of the underlying Rust code.
//
// This makes a cleaner a better alternative to _not_ calling `destroy()` as
// and when the object is finished with, but the abstraction is not perfect: if the Rust object's `drop`
// method is slow, and/or there are many objects to cleanup, and it's on a low end Android device, then the cleaner
// thread may be starved, and the app will leak memory.
//
// In this case, `destroy`ing manually may be a better solution.
//
// The cleaner can live side by side with the manual calling of `destroy`. In the order of responsiveness, uniffi objects
// with Rust peers are reclaimed:
//
// 1. By calling the `destroy` method of the object, which calls `rustObject.free()`. If that doesn't happen:
// 2. When the object becomes unreachable, AND the Cleaner thread gets to call `rustObject.free()`. If the thread is starved then:
// 3. The memory is reclaimed when the process terminates.
//
// [1] https://stackoverflow.com/questions/24376768/can-java-finalize-an-object-when-it-is-still-in-scope/24380219
//


/**
 * The cleaner interface for Object finalization code to run.
 * This is the entry point to any implementation that we're using.
 *
 * The cleaner registers objects and returns cleanables, so now we are
 * defining a `UniffiCleaner` with a `UniffiClenaer.Cleanable` to abstract the
 * different implmentations available at compile time.
 *
 * @suppress
 */
interface UniffiCleaner {
    interface Cleanable {
        fun clean()
    }

    fun register(value: Any, cleanUpTask: Runnable): UniffiCleaner.Cleanable

    companion object
}

// The fallback Jna cleaner, which is available for both Android, and the JVM.
private class UniffiJnaCleaner : UniffiCleaner {
    private val cleaner = com.sun.jna.internal.Cleaner.getCleaner()

    override fun register(value: Any, cleanUpTask: Runnable): UniffiCleaner.Cleanable =
        UniffiJnaCleanable(cleaner.register(value, cleanUpTask))
}

private class UniffiJnaCleanable(
    private val cleanable: com.sun.jna.internal.Cleaner.Cleanable,
) : UniffiCleaner.Cleanable {
    override fun clean() = cleanable.clean()
}

// We decide at uniffi binding generation time whether we were
// using Android or not.
// There are further runtime checks to chose the correct implementation
// of the cleaner.
private fun UniffiCleaner.Companion.create(): UniffiCleaner =
    try {
        // For safety's sake: if the library hasn't been run in android_cleaner = true
        // mode, but is being run on Android, then we still need to think about
        // Android API versions.
        // So we check if java.lang.ref.Cleaner is there, and use that…
        java.lang.Class.forName("java.lang.ref.Cleaner")
        JavaLangRefCleaner()
    } catch (e: ClassNotFoundException) {
        // … otherwise, fallback to the JNA cleaner.
        UniffiJnaCleaner()
    }

private class JavaLangRefCleaner : UniffiCleaner {
    val cleaner = java.lang.ref.Cleaner.create()

    override fun register(value: Any, cleanUpTask: Runnable): UniffiCleaner.Cleanable =
        JavaLangRefCleanable(cleaner.register(value, cleanUpTask))
}

private class JavaLangRefCleanable(
    val cleanable: java.lang.ref.Cleaner.Cleanable
) : UniffiCleaner.Cleanable {
    override fun clean() = cleanable.clean()
}

    fun `listWallpapers`(): List<WallpaperMetadata>
    
    fun `pairWithServer`(`serverUrl`: kotlin.String, `token`: kotlin.String, `deviceName`: kotlin.String, `screenW`: kotlin.UInt, `screenH`: kotlin.UInt): PairingResult
    
    fun `renderWallpaper`(`screenName`: kotlin.String, `targetW`: kotlin.UInt, `targetH`: kotlin.UInt, `format`: kotlin.String): kotlin.String
    
    fun `setWallpaper`(`screenName`: kotlin.String, `imageHash`: kotlin.String, `fit`: kotlin.String): kotlin.String
    
    fun `syncWithPeer`(`peerUrl`: kotlin.String): SyncReport
    
    companion object
}

open class SyncWallEngineBinding: Disposable, AutoCloseable, SyncWallEngineBindingInterface {

    constructor(pointer: Pointer) {
        this.pointer = pointer
        this.cleanable = UniffiLib.CLEANER.register(this, UniffiCleanAction(pointer))
    }

    /**
     * This constructor can be used to instantiate a fake object. Only used for tests. Any
     * attempt to actually use an object constructed this way will fail as there is no
     * connected Rust object.
     */
    @Suppress("UNUSED_PARAMETER")
    constructor(noPointer: NoPointer) {
        this.pointer = null
        this.cleanable = UniffiLib.CLEANER.register(this, UniffiCleanAction(pointer))
    }
    constructor(`baseDir`: kotlin.String, `deviceName`: kotlin.String) :
        this(
    uniffiRustCallWithError(SyncWallFfiException) { _status ->
    UniffiLib.INSTANCE.uniffi_sync_wall_core_fn_constructor_syncwallenginebinding_new(
        FfiConverterString.lower(`baseDir`),FfiConverterString.lower(`deviceName`),_status)
}
    )

    protected val pointer: Pointer?
    protected val cleanable: UniffiCleaner.Cleanable

    private val wasDestroyed = AtomicBoolean(false)
    private val callCounter = AtomicLong(1)

    override fun destroy() {
        // Only allow a single call to this method.
        // TODO: maybe we should log a warning if called more than once?
        if (this.wasDestroyed.compareAndSet(false, true)) {
            // This decrement always matches the initial count of 1 given at creation time.
            if (this.callCounter.decrementAndGet() == 0L) {
                cleanable.clean()
            }
        }
    }

    @Synchronized
    override fun close() {
        this.destroy()
    }

    internal inline fun <R> callWithPointer(block: (ptr: Pointer) -> R): R {
        // Check and increment the call counter, to keep the object alive.
        // This needs a compare-and-set retry loop in case of concurrent updates.
        do {
            val c = this.callCounter.get()
            if (c == 0L) {
                throw IllegalStateException("${this.javaClass.simpleName} object has already been destroyed")
            }
            if (c == Long.MAX_VALUE) {
                throw IllegalStateException("${this.javaClass.simpleName} call counter would overflow")
            }
        } while (! this.callCounter.compareAndSet(c, c + 1L))
        // Now we can safely do the method call without the pointer being freed concurrently.
        try {
            return block(this.uniffiClonePointer())
        } finally {
            // This decrement always matches the increment we performed above.
            if (this.callCounter.decrementAndGet() == 0L) {
                cleanable.clean()
            }
        }
    }

    // Use a static inner class instead of a closure so as not to accidentally
    // capture `this` as part of the cleanable's action.
    private class UniffiCleanAction(private val pointer: Pointer?) : Runnable {
        override fun run() {
            pointer?.let { ptr ->
                uniffiRustCall { status ->
                    UniffiLib.INSTANCE.uniffi_sync_wall_core_fn_free_syncwallenginebinding(ptr, status)
                }
            }
        }
    }

    fun uniffiClonePointer(): Pointer {
        return uniffiRustCall() { status ->
            UniffiLib.INSTANCE.uniffi_sync_wall_core_fn_clone_syncwallenginebinding(pointer!!, status)
        }
    }

    
    @Throws(SyncWallFfiException::class)override fun `fetchBingDaily`(): WallpaperMetadata {
            return FfiConverterTypeWallpaperMetadata.lift(
    callWithPointer {
    uniffiRustCallWithError(SyncWallFfiException) { _status ->
    UniffiLib.INSTANCE.uniffi_sync_wall_core_fn_method_syncwallenginebinding_fetch_bing_daily(
        it, _status)
}
    }
    )
    }
    

    override fun `getActiveWallpaperPath`(`screenName`: kotlin.String): kotlin.String? {
            return FfiConverterOptionalString.lift(
    callWithPointer {
    uniffiRustCall() { _status ->

        it, FfiConverterString.lower(`screenName`),_status)

/**
 * @suppress
 */
public object FfiConverterTypeSyncWallEngineBinding: FfiConverter<SyncWallEngineBinding, Pointer> {

    override fun lower(value: SyncWallEngineBinding): Pointer {
        return value.uniffiClonePointer()
    }

    override fun lift(value: Pointer): SyncWallEngineBinding {
        return SyncWallEngineBinding(value)
    }

    override fun read(buf: ByteBuffer): SyncWallEngineBinding {
        // The Rust code always writes pointers as 8 bytes, and will
        // fail to compile if they don't fit.
        return lift(Pointer(buf.getLong()))
    }

    override fun allocationSize(value: SyncWallEngineBinding) = 8UL

    override fun write(value: SyncWallEngineBinding, buf: ByteBuffer) {
        // The Rust code always expects pointers written as 8 bytes,
        // and will fail to compile if they don't fit.
        buf.putLong(Pointer.nativeValue(lower(value)))
    }
}



data class PairingResult (
    var `success`: kotlin.Boolean,

/**
 * @suppress
 */
public object FfiConverterTypePairingResult: FfiConverterRustBuffer<PairingResult> {

        return PairingResult(
            FfiConverterBoolean.read(buf),
            FfiConverterString.read(buf),
            FfiConverterString.read(buf),
        )
    }

    override fun allocationSize(value: PairingResult) = (
            FfiConverterBoolean.allocationSize(value.`success`) +
            FfiConverterString.allocationSize(value.`serverName`) +
            FfiConverterString.allocationSize(value.`serverFingerprint`)
    )

    override fun write(value: PairingResult, buf: ByteBuffer) {
            FfiConverterBoolean.write(value.`success`, buf)
            FfiConverterString.write(value.`serverName`, buf)
            FfiConverterString.write(value.`serverFingerprint`, buf)
    }
}

sealed class SyncWallFfiException: kotlin.Exception() {
    
    class Internal(
        
        val `msg`: kotlin.String
        ) : SyncWallFfiException() {
        override val message
            get() = "msg=${ `msg` }"
    }
    
    class Io(
        
        val `msg`: kotlin.String
        ) : SyncWallFfiException() {
        override val message
            get() = "msg=${ `msg` }"
    }
    
    class NotFound(
        
        val `msg`: kotlin.String
        ) : SyncWallFfiException() {
        override val message
            get() = "msg=${ `msg` }"
    }
    
    class Network(
        
        val `msg`: kotlin.String
        ) : SyncWallFfiException() {
        override val message
            get() = "msg=${ `msg` }"
    }
    
    class PairingFailed(
        
        val `msg`: kotlin.String
        ) : SyncWallFfiException() {
        override val message
            get() = "msg=${ `msg` }"
    }
    
    class SyncFailed(
        
        val `msg`: kotlin.String
        ) : SyncWallFfiException() {
        override val message
            get() = "msg=${ `msg` }"
    }
    

    companion object ErrorHandler : UniffiRustCallStatusErrorHandler<SyncWallFfiException> {
        override fun lift(error_buf: RustBuffer.ByValue): SyncWallFfiException = FfiConverterTypeSyncWallFfiError.lift(error_buf)
    }

    
}

/**
 * @suppress
 */
public object FfiConverterTypeSyncWallFfiError : FfiConverterRustBuffer<SyncWallFfiException> {
    override fun read(buf: ByteBuffer): SyncWallFfiException {
        

        return when(buf.getInt()) {
            1 -> SyncWallFfiException.Internal(
                FfiConverterString.read(buf),
                )
            2 -> SyncWallFfiException.Io(
                FfiConverterString.read(buf),
                )
            3 -> SyncWallFfiException.NotFound(
                FfiConverterString.read(buf),
                )
            4 -> SyncWallFfiException.Network(
                FfiConverterString.read(buf),
                )
            5 -> SyncWallFfiException.PairingFailed(
                FfiConverterString.read(buf),
                )
            6 -> SyncWallFfiException.SyncFailed(
                FfiConverterString.read(buf),
                )
            else -> throw RuntimeException("invalid error enum value, something is very wrong!!")
        }
    }

    override fun allocationSize(value: SyncWallFfiException): ULong {
        return when(value) {
            is SyncWallFfiException.Internal -> (
                // Add the size for the Int that specifies the variant plus the size needed for all fields
                4UL
                + FfiConverterString.allocationSize(value.`msg`)
            )
            is SyncWallFfiException.Io -> (
                // Add the size for the Int that specifies the variant plus the size needed for all fields
                4UL
                + FfiConverterString.allocationSize(value.`msg`)
            )
            is SyncWallFfiException.NotFound -> (
                // Add the size for the Int that specifies the variant plus the size needed for all fields
                4UL
                + FfiConverterString.allocationSize(value.`msg`)
            )
            is SyncWallFfiException.Network -> (
                // Add the size for the Int that specifies the variant plus the size needed for all fields
                4UL
                + FfiConverterString.allocationSize(value.`msg`)
            )
            is SyncWallFfiException.PairingFailed -> (
                // Add the size for the Int that specifies the variant plus the size needed for all fields
                4UL
                + FfiConverterString.allocationSize(value.`msg`)
            )
            is SyncWallFfiException.SyncFailed -> (
                // Add the size for the Int that specifies the variant plus the size needed for all fields
                4UL
                + FfiConverterString.allocationSize(value.`msg`)
            )
        }
    }

    override fun write(value: SyncWallFfiException, buf: ByteBuffer) {
        when(value) {
            is SyncWallFfiException.Internal -> {
                buf.putInt(1)
                FfiConverterString.write(value.`msg`, buf)
                Unit
            }
            is SyncWallFfiException.Io -> {
                buf.putInt(2)
                FfiConverterString.write(value.`msg`, buf)
                Unit
            }
            is SyncWallFfiException.NotFound -> {
                buf.putInt(3)
                FfiConverterString.write(value.`msg`, buf)
                Unit
            }
            is SyncWallFfiException.Network -> {
                buf.putInt(4)
                FfiConverterString.write(value.`msg`, buf)
                Unit
            }
            is SyncWallFfiException.PairingFailed -> {
                buf.putInt(5)
                FfiConverterString.write(value.`msg`, buf)
                Unit
            }
            is SyncWallFfiException.SyncFailed -> {
                buf.putInt(6)
                FfiConverterString.write(value.`msg`, buf)
                Unit
            }
        }.let { /* this makes the `when` an expression, which ensures it is exhaustive */ }
    }

}




/**
 * @suppress
 */
public object FfiConverterOptionalString: FfiConverterRustBuffer<kotlin.String?> {
    override fun read(buf: ByteBuffer): kotlin.String? {
        if (buf.get().toInt() == 0) {
            return null
        }
        return FfiConverterString.read(buf)
    }

    override fun allocationSize(value: kotlin.String?): ULong {
        if (value == null) {
            return 1UL
        } else {
            return 1UL + FfiConverterString.allocationSize(value)
        }
    }

    override fun write(value: kotlin.String?, buf: ByteBuffer) {
        if (value == null) {
            buf.put(0)
        } else {
            buf.put(1)
            FfiConverterString.write(value, buf)
        }
    }
}




/**
 * @suppress
 */
public object FfiConverterSequenceTypeWallpaperMetadata: FfiConverterRustBuffer<List<WallpaperMetadata>> {
    override fun read(buf: ByteBuffer): List<WallpaperMetadata> {
        val len = buf.getInt()
        return List<WallpaperMetadata>(len) {
            FfiConverterTypeWallpaperMetadata.read(buf)
        }
    }

    override fun allocationSize(value: List<WallpaperMetadata>): ULong {
        val sizeForLength = 4UL
        val sizeForItems = value.map { FfiConverterTypeWallpaperMetadata.allocationSize(it) }.sum()
        return sizeForLength + sizeForItems
    }

    override fun write(value: List<WallpaperMetadata>, buf: ByteBuffer) {
        buf.putInt(value.size)
        value.iterator().forEach {
            FfiConverterTypeWallpaperMetadata.write(it, buf)
        }
    }
}

```

```
#!/usr/bin/env bash

##############################################################################
##
##  Gradle start up script for UN*X
##
##############################################################################

# Add default JVM options here. You can also use JAVA_OPTS and GRADLE_OPTS to pass JVM options to this script.
DEFAULT_JVM_OPTS=""

APP_NAME="Gradle"
APP_BASE_NAME=`basename "$0"`

# Use the maximum available, or set MAX_FD != -1 to use that value.
MAX_FD="maximum"

warn ( ) {
    echo "$*"
}

die ( ) {
    echo
    echo "$*"
    echo
    exit 1
}

# OS specific support (must be 'true' or 'false').
cygwin=false
msys=false
darwin=false
case "`uname`" in
  CYGWIN* )
    cygwin=true
    ;;
  Darwin* )
    darwin=true
    ;;
  MINGW* )
    msys=true
    ;;
esac

# Attempt to set APP_HOME
# Resolve links: $0 may be a link
PRG="$0"
# Need this for relative symlinks.
while [ -h "$PRG" ] ; do
    ls=`ls -ld "$PRG"`
    link=`expr "$ls" : '.*-> \(.*\)$'`
    if expr "$link" : '/.*' > /dev/null; then
        PRG="$link"
    else
        PRG=`dirname "$PRG"`"/$link"
    fi
done
SAVED="`pwd`"
cd "`dirname \"$PRG\"`/" >/dev/null
APP_HOME="`pwd -P`"
cd "$SAVED" >/dev/null

CLASSPATH=$APP_HOME/gradle/wrapper/gradle-wrapper.jar

# Determine the Java command to use to start the JVM.
if [ -n "$JAVA_HOME" ] ; then
    if [ -x "$JAVA_HOME/jre/sh/java" ] ; then
        # IBM's JDK on AIX uses strange locations for the executables
        JAVACMD="$JAVA_HOME/jre/sh/java"
    else
        JAVACMD="$JAVA_HOME/bin/java"
    fi
    if [ ! -x "$JAVACMD" ] ; then
        die "ERROR: JAVA_HOME is set to an invalid directory: $JAVA_HOME

Please set the JAVA_HOME variable in your environment to match the
location of your Java installation."
    fi
else
    JAVACMD="java"
    which java >/dev/null 2>&1 || die "ERROR: JAVA_HOME is not set and no 'java' command could be found in your PATH.

Please set the JAVA_HOME variable in your environment to match the
location of your Java installation."
fi

# Increase the maximum file descriptors if we can.
if [ "$cygwin" = "false" -a "$darwin" = "false" ] ; then
    MAX_FD_LIMIT=`ulimit -H -n`
    if [ $? -eq 0 ] ; then
        if [ "$MAX_FD" = "maximum" -o "$MAX_FD" = "max" ] ; then
            MAX_FD="$MAX_FD_LIMIT"
        fi
        ulimit -n $MAX_FD
        if [ $? -ne 0 ] ; then
            warn "Could not set maximum file descriptor limit: $MAX_FD"
        fi
    else
        warn "Could not query maximum file descriptor limit: $MAX_FD_LIMIT"
    fi
fi

# For Darwin, add options to specify how the application appears in the dock
if $darwin; then
    GRADLE_OPTS="$GRADLE_OPTS \"-Xdock:name=$APP_NAME\" \"-Xdock:icon=$APP_HOME/media/gradle.icns\""
fi

# For Cygwin, switch paths to Windows format before running java
if $cygwin ; then
    APP_HOME=`cygpath --path --mixed "$APP_HOME"`
    CLASSPATH=`cygpath --path --mixed "$CLASSPATH"`
    JAVACMD=`cygpath --unix "$JAVACMD"`

    # We build the pattern for arguments to be converted via cygpath
    ROOTDIRSRAW=`find -L / -maxdepth 1 -mindepth 1 -type d 2>/dev/null`
    SEP=""
    for dir in $ROOTDIRSRAW ; do
        ROOTDIRS="$ROOTDIRS$SEP$dir"
        SEP="|"
    done
    OURCYGPATTERN="(^($ROOTDIRS))"
    # Add a user-defined pattern to the cygpath arguments
    if [ "$GRADLE_CYGPATTERN" != "" ] ; then
        OURCYGPATTERN="$OURCYGPATTERN|($GRADLE_CYGPATTERN)"
    fi
    # Now convert the arguments - kludge to limit ourselves to /bin/sh
    i=0
    for arg in "$@" ; do
        CHECK=`echo "$arg"|egrep -c "$OURCYGPATTERN" -`
        CHECK2=`echo "$arg"|egrep -c "^-"`                                 ### Determine if an option

        if [ $CHECK -ne 0 ] && [ $CHECK2 -eq 0 ] ; then                    ### Added a condition
            eval `echo args$i`=`cygpath --path --ignore --mixed "$arg"`
        else
            eval `echo args$i`="\"$arg\""
        fi
        i=$((i+1))
    done
    case $i in
        (0) set -- ;;
        (1) set -- "$args0" ;;
        (2) set -- "$args0" "$args1" ;;
        (3) set -- "$args0" "$args1" "$args2" ;;
        (4) set -- "$args0" "$args1" "$args2" "$args3" ;;
        (5) set -- "$args0" "$args1" "$args2" "$args3" "$args4" ;;
        (6) set -- "$args0" "$args1" "$args2" "$args3" "$args4" "$args5" ;;
        (7) set -- "$args0" "$args1" "$args2" "$args3" "$args4" "$args5" "$args6" ;;
        (8) set -- "$args0" "$args1" "$args2" "$args3" "$args4" "$args5" "$args6" "$args7" ;;
        (9) set -- "$args0" "$args1" "$args2" "$args3" "$args4" "$args5" "$args6" "$args7" "$args8" ;;
    esac
fi

# Split up the JVM_OPTS And GRADLE_OPTS values into an array, following the shell quoting and substitution rules
function splitJvmOpts() {
    JVM_OPTS=("$@")
}
eval splitJvmOpts $DEFAULT_JVM_OPTS $JAVA_OPTS $GRADLE_OPTS
JVM_OPTS[${#JVM_OPTS[*]}]="-Dorg.gradle.appname=$APP_BASE_NAME"

exec "$JAVACMD" "${JVM_OPTS[@]}" -classpath "$CLASSPATH" org.gradle.wrapper.GradleWrapperMain "$@"
```

```rust
use std::collections::HashSet;
use std::fs::{self, File};
use std::io::{Cursor, Write};
use std::path::{Path, PathBuf};
use std::sync::Mutex;
use fast_image_resize as fr;
use image::{DynamicImage, GenericImageView, ImageFormat};
use thiserror::Error;

use crate::model::{DerivativeSpec, Fit, Hash, OutFormat};

#[derive(Error, Debug)]
pub enum CropError {
    #[error("Image error: {0}")]
    Image(#[from] image::ImageError),
    #[error("IO error: {0}")]
    Io(#[from] std::io::Error),
    #[error("Image too large: {0} pixels (max 64MP)")]
    TooLarge(u64),
    #[error("Invalid dimensions: {0}x{1}")]
    InvalidDimensions(u32, u32),
    #[error("Source not found: {0}")]
    NotFound(String),
}

pub type Result<T> = std::result::Result<T, CropError>;

pub struct CropEngine {
    derived_dir: PathBuf,
    in_flight: Mutex<HashSet<String>>,
}

impl CropEngine {
    pub fn new<P: AsRef<Path>>(derived_dir: P) -> Self {
        Self {
            derived_dir: derived_dir.as_ref().to_path_buf(),
            in_flight: Mutex::new(HashSet::new()),
        }
    }

    pub fn derivative_path(&self, source_hash: &Hash, spec: &DerivativeSpec) -> PathBuf {
        let hex = source_hash.to_hex();
        let prefix = &hex[..2];
        let key = spec.cache_key(source_hash);
        self.derived_dir.join(prefix).join(&hex).join(key)
    }

    /// Read source image, apply EXIF orientation, crop, resize with fast_image_resize, and cache.
    pub fn get_or_render<P: AsRef<Path>>(
        &self,
        source_hash: &Hash,
        source_path: P,
        spec: &DerivativeSpec,
    ) -> Result<PathBuf> {
        let dest = self.derivative_path(source_hash, spec);

        // Fast cache hit
        if dest.exists() && fs::metadata(&dest).map(|m| m.len() > 0).unwrap_or(false) {
            return Ok(dest);
        }

        let key = spec.cache_key(source_hash);
        // Single-flight deduplication
        {
            let mut inflight = self.in_flight.lock().unwrap();
            while inflight.contains(&key) {
                drop(inflight);
                std::thread::sleep(std::time::Duration::from_millis(20));
                if dest.exists() {
                    return Ok(dest);
                }
                inflight = self.in_flight.lock().unwrap();
            }
            inflight.insert(key.clone());
        }

        struct InFlightGuard<'a> {
            set: &'a Mutex<HashSet<String>>,
            key: String,
        }
        impl<'a> Drop for InFlightGuard<'a> {
            fn drop(&mut self) {
                let mut lock = self.set.lock().unwrap();
                lock.remove(&self.key);
            }
        }
        let _guard = InFlightGuard {
            set: &self.in_flight,
            key,
        };

        let source_path = source_path.as_ref();
        if !source_path.exists() {
            return Err(CropError::NotFound(source_path.display().to_string()));
        }

        let source_bytes = fs::read(source_path)?;
        let mut img = image::load_from_memory(&source_bytes)?;

        // Cap decoded memory to 64 MP (prevent OOM)
        let total_pixels = (img.width() as u64) * (img.height() as u64);
        if total_pixels > 64_000_000 {
            return Err(CropError::TooLarge(total_pixels));
        }

        // Apply EXIF orientation
        img = apply_exif_orientation(&source_bytes, img);

        let (target_w, target_h) = spec.target;
        if target_w == 0 || target_h == 0 {
            return Err(CropError::InvalidDimensions(target_w, target_h));
        }

        // Step 1: Crop first to target aspect ratio (saves CPU prior to resize)
        let cropped = crop_to_aspect(&img, target_w, target_h, spec.fit, spec.focus);

        // Step 2: SIMD resize using fast_image_resize
        let resized = resize_simd(&cropped, target_w, target_h)?;

        // Step 3: Encode to specified format
        let encoded_bytes = encode_image(&resized, spec.format)?;

        // Step 4: Atomic write (.tmp -> rename)
        if let Some(parent) = dest.parent() {
            fs::create_dir_all(parent)?;
        }
        let tmp_path = dest.with_extension("tmp");
        let mut file = File::create(&tmp_path)?;
        file.write_all(&encoded_bytes)?;
        file.sync_all()?;
        fs::rename(tmp_path, &dest)?;

        Ok(dest)
    }
}

/// Parse EXIF tag from bytes and rotate/flip DynamicImage accordingly
fn apply_exif_orientation(bytes: &[u8], img: DynamicImage) -> DynamicImage {
    let mut cursor = Cursor::new(bytes);
    if let Ok(exif_reader) = exif::Reader::new().read_from_container(&mut cursor) {
        if let Some(field) = exif_reader.get_field(exif::Tag::Orientation, exif::In::PRIMARY) {
            if let Some(val) = field.value.get_uint(0) {
                return match val {
                    1 => img,
                    2 => img.fliph(),
                    3 => img.rotate180(),
                    4 => img.flipv(),
                    5 => img.rotate90().fliph(),
                    6 => img.rotate90(),
                    7 => img.rotate270().fliph(),
                    8 => img.rotate270(),
                    _ => img,
                };
            }
        }
    }
    img
}

/// Crop source image to match target aspect ratio (W/H) based on Fit mode and focus point
fn crop_to_aspect(
    img: &DynamicImage,
    target_w: u32,
    target_h: u32,
    fit: Fit,
    focus: Option<(f32, f32)>,
) -> DynamicImage {
    let (src_w, src_h) = img.dimensions();
    let src_aspect = src_w as f64 / src_h as f64;
    let target_aspect = target_w as f64 / target_h as f64;

    match fit {
        Fit::Fill | Fit::CropCenter => {
            let focus = if fit == Fit::CropCenter {
                (0.5, 0.5)
            } else {
                focus.unwrap_or((0.5, 0.5))
            };

            let (crop_w, crop_h, crop_x, crop_y) = if src_aspect > target_aspect {
                // Source is wider than target -> crop horizontally
                let crop_w = (src_h as f64 * target_aspect).round() as u32;
                let crop_h = src_h;
                let max_x = src_w.saturating_sub(crop_w);
                let center_x = (focus.0 * src_w as f32) as i32;
                let x = (center_x - (crop_w as i32 / 2)).clamp(0, max_x as i32) as u32;
                (crop_w, crop_h, x, 0)
            } else {
                // Source is taller than target -> crop vertically
                let crop_w = src_w;
                let crop_h = (src_w as f64 / target_aspect).round() as u32;
                let max_y = src_h.saturating_sub(crop_h);
                let center_y = (focus.1 * src_h as f32) as i32;
                let y = (center_y - (crop_h as i32 / 2)).clamp(0, max_y as i32) as u32;
                (crop_w, crop_h, 0, y)
            };

            img.crop_imm(crop_x, crop_y, crop_w.min(src_w), crop_h.min(src_h))
        }
        Fit::CropRect { x, y, w, h } => {
            let crop_x = (x * src_w as f32) as u32;
            let crop_y = (y * src_h as f32) as u32;
            let crop_w = (w * src_w as f32) as u32;
            let crop_h = (h * src_h as f32) as u32;
            img.crop_imm(
                crop_x.min(src_w),
                crop_y.min(src_h),
                crop_w.min(src_w.saturating_sub(crop_x)),
                crop_h.min(src_h.saturating_sub(crop_y)),
            )
        }
        Fit::Fit => {
            // Letterbox/Fit: do not crop source, resize directly preserving aspect ratio
            img.clone()
        }
    }
}

/// Perform fast SIMD Lanczos3 resize using fast_image_resize
fn resize_simd(img: &DynamicImage, target_w: u32, target_h: u32) -> Result<DynamicImage> {
    let rgba = img.to_rgba8();
    let (src_w, src_h) = rgba.dimensions();

    let src_image = fr::images::Image::from_vec_u8(
        src_w,
        src_h,
        rgba.into_raw(),
        fr::PixelType::U8x4,
    ).map_err(|e| CropError::Image(image::ImageError::Decoding(image::error::DecodingError::new(
        image::error::ImageFormatHint::Unknown,
        e.to_string(),
    ))))?;

    let mut dst_image = fr::images::Image::new(target_w, target_h, fr::PixelType::U8x4);
    let mut resizer = fr::Resizer::new();
    resizer.resize(&src_image, &mut dst_image, &fr::ResizeOptions::default())
        .map_err(|e| CropError::Image(image::ImageError::Decoding(image::error::DecodingError::new(
            image::error::ImageFormatHint::Unknown,
            e.to_string(),
        ))))?;

    let buffer = dst_image.into_vec();
    let img_buffer = image::RgbaImage::from_raw(target_w, target_h, buffer)
        .ok_or_else(|| CropError::InvalidDimensions(target_w, target_h))?;

    Ok(DynamicImage::ImageRgba8(img_buffer))
}

fn encode_image(img: &DynamicImage, format: OutFormat) -> Result<Vec<u8>> {
    let mut bytes = Vec::new();
    let mut cursor = Cursor::new(&mut bytes);

    match format {
        OutFormat::Png => {
            img.write_to(&mut cursor, ImageFormat::Png)?;
        }
        OutFormat::WebpLossy(_) => {
            // WebP encoding via image crate webp feature
            img.write_to(&mut cursor, ImageFormat::WebP)?;
        }
    }

    Ok(bytes)
}

#[cfg(test)]
mod tests {
    use super::*;
    use tempfile::tempdir;

    fn create_test_image(w: u32, h: u32) -> DynamicImage {
        let mut img = image::RgbaImage::new(w, h);
        for pixel in img.pixels_mut() {
            *pixel = image::Rgba([100, 150, 200, 255]);
        }
        DynamicImage::ImageRgba8(img)
    }

    #[test]
    fn test_crop_portrait_to_landscape() {
        let dir = tempdir().unwrap();
        let engine = CropEngine::new(dir.path().join("derived"));

        let src = create_test_image(1080, 1920); // Portrait (9:16)
        let src_path = dir.path().join("portrait.png");
        src.save(&src_path).unwrap();

        let hash = Hash::compute(b"portrait_img");
        let spec = DerivativeSpec {
            target: (1920, 1080), // Landscape (16:9)
            fit: Fit::Fill,
            focus: Some((0.5, 0.5)),
            format: OutFormat::Png,
        };

        let result_path = engine.get_or_render(&hash, &src_path, &spec).unwrap();
        assert!(result_path.exists());

        let out = image::open(&result_path).unwrap();
        assert_eq!(out.width(), 1920);
        assert_eq!(out.height(), 1080);
    }

    #[test]
    fn test_crop_landscape_to_portrait() {
        let dir = tempdir().unwrap();
        let engine = CropEngine::new(dir.path().join("derived"));

        let src = create_test_image(2560, 1440); // Landscape
        let src_path = dir.path().join("landscape.png");
        src.save(&src_path).unwrap();

        let hash = Hash::compute(b"landscape_img");
        let spec = DerivativeSpec {
            target: (1080, 2400), // Phone screen (portrait)
            fit: Fit::Fill,
            focus: Some((0.5, 0.2)), // Top-weighted focus
            format: OutFormat::Png,
        };

        let result_path = engine.get_or_render(&hash, &src_path, &spec).unwrap();
        assert!(result_path.exists());

        let out = image::open(&result_path).unwrap();
        assert_eq!(out.width(), 1080);
        assert_eq!(out.height(), 2400);
    }
}
```

```rust
pub mod crop;
pub mod model;
pub mod net;
pub mod source;
pub mod store;
pub mod uniffi_api;

uniffi::setup_scaffolding!("sync_wall_core");

use std::fs;
use std::path::{Path, PathBuf};
use std::sync::Arc;
use thiserror::Error;

use crate::crop::CropEngine;
use crate::model::*;
use crate::net::DeviceIdentity;
#[cfg(feature = "server")]
use crate::net::ServerState;
use crate::source::{SourceError, SourceManager, UnsplashConfig};
use crate::store::Store;

#[derive(Error, Debug)]
pub enum EngineError {
    #[error("Store error: {0}")]
    Store(#[from] store::StoreError),
    #[error("Crop error: {0}")]
    Crop(#[from] crop::CropError),
    #[error("Network error: {0}")]
    Net(#[from] net::NetError),
    #[error("Source error: {0}")]
    Source(#[from] SourceError),
    #[error("Reqwest error: {0}")]
    Reqwest(#[from] reqwest::Error),
    #[error("IO error: {0}")]
    Io(#[from] std::io::Error),
    #[error("Not found: {0}")]
    NotFound(String),
}

pub type Result<T> = std::result::Result<T, EngineError>;

/// Deep engine facade unifying storage, cropping, synchronization, and local identity.
#[derive(Clone)]
pub struct SyncWallEngine {
    base_dir: PathBuf,
    store: Arc<Store>,
    crop: Arc<CropEngine>,
    identity: DeviceIdentity,
    active_token: Arc<parking_lot::Mutex<Option<crate::net::PairingSession>>>,
}

impl SyncWallEngine {
    pub fn open<P: AsRef<Path>>(base_dir: P, device_name: &str) -> Result<Self> {
        let base_dir = base_dir.as_ref().to_path_buf();
        fs::create_dir_all(&base_dir)?;

        let id_dir = base_dir.join("identity");
        let identity = match DeviceIdentity::load_from_dir(&id_dir)? {
            Some(id) => id,
            None => {
                let id = DeviceIdentity::generate(device_name)?;
                id.save_to_dir(&id_dir)?;
                id
            }
        };

        let store = Arc::new(Store::open(&base_dir, &identity.fingerprint)?);
        let crop = Arc::new(CropEngine::new(base_dir.join("derived")));
        let active_token = Arc::new(parking_lot::Mutex::new(None));

        Ok(Self {
            base_dir,
            store,
            crop,
            identity,
            active_token,
        })
    }

    pub fn base_dir(&self) -> &Path {
        &self.base_dir
    }

    pub fn device_id(&self) -> &str {
        &self.identity.fingerprint
    }

    pub fn store(&self) -> &Arc<Store> {
        &self.store
    }

    pub fn crop(&self) -> &Arc<CropEngine> {
        &self.crop
    }

    pub fn identity(&self) -> &DeviceIdentity {
        &self.identity
    }

    #[cfg(feature = "server")]
    pub fn server_state(&self) -> ServerState {
        ServerState {
            store: self.store.clone(),
            crop: self.crop.clone(),
            identity: self.identity.clone(),
            active_pairing_token: self.active_token.clone(),
        }
    }

    /// Ingest a local image file into CAS and catalog it in SQLite
    pub fn ingest_file<P: AsRef<Path>>(&self, path: P, tags: Vec<String>) -> Result<ImageRecord> {
        let path = path.as_ref();
        let bytes = fs::read(path)?;
        let filename = path.file_name()
            .and_then(|n| n.to_str())
            .unwrap_or("image.png");
        let record = self.store.ingest_bytes(&bytes, filename, tags)?;
        Ok(record)
    }

    pub fn ingest_bytes(&self, bytes: &[u8], name: &str, tags: Vec<String>) -> Result<ImageRecord> {
        let record = self.store.ingest_bytes(bytes, name, tags)?;
        Ok(record)
    }

    pub fn get_image(&self, hash: &Hash) -> Result<Option<ImageRecord>> {
        let res = self.store.get_image(hash)?;
        Ok(res)
    }

    pub fn list_images(&self) -> Result<Vec<ImageRecord>> {
        let res = self.store.list_images()?;
        Ok(res)
    }

    /// Assign wallpaper to local screen
    pub fn set_wallpaper(
        &self,
        screen_name: &str,
        image_hash: Hash,
        fit: Fit,
        focus: Option<(f32, f32)>,
    ) -> Result<AssignmentRecord> {
        let assign = self.store.set_assignment(
            self.device_id(),
            screen_name,
            image_hash,
            fit,
            focus,
        )?;
        Ok(assign)
    }

    /// Render or fetch cached derivative for an assignment and promote to stable current/<screen_name>.<ext>
    /// This guarantees that when LRU cache evicts files from derived/, the desktop never loses its active wallpaper.
    pub fn render_wallpaper_for_screen(
        &self,
        screen_name: &str,
        target_dim: (u32, u32),
        format: OutFormat,
    ) -> Result<PathBuf> {
        let assign = self.store.get_assignment(self.device_id(), screen_name)?
            .ok_or_else(|| EngineError::NotFound(format!("No assignment for screen {}", screen_name)))?;

        let source_path = self.store.blob_path(&assign.image_hash);
        if !source_path.exists() {
            return Err(EngineError::NotFound(format!("Source image {} missing from CAS", assign.image_hash)));
        }

        let spec = DerivativeSpec {
            target: target_dim,
            fit: assign.fit,
            focus: assign.focus,
            format,
        };

        let cached_path = self.crop.get_or_render(&assign.image_hash, &source_path, &spec)?;

        // Fix 2: Copy rendered file to stable current/<screen_name>.<ext> via atomic temp file
        let ext = match format {
            OutFormat::Png => "png",
            OutFormat::WebpLossy(_) => "webp",
        };
        let current_dir = self.base_dir.join("current");
        fs::create_dir_all(&current_dir)?;
        let stable_path = current_dir.join(format!("{}.{}", screen_name, ext));
        let tmp_path = current_dir.join(format!("{}.{}.tmp", screen_name, ext));

        fs::copy(&cached_path, &tmp_path)?;
        fs::rename(&tmp_path, &stable_path)?;

        Ok(stable_path)
    }

    /// Generate a one-time pairing session with 128-bit cryptographically secure token and 6-digit PIN fallback
    pub fn generate_pairing_session(&self) -> crate::net::PairingSession {
        let session = crate::net::PairingSession::new();
        *self.active_token.lock() = Some(session.clone());
        session
    }

    pub fn generate_pairing_token(&self) -> String {
        self.generate_pairing_session().token
    }

    /// Run sync against a peer URL
    pub async fn sync_with_peer(&self, peer_url: &str) -> Result<ApplyReport> {
        let client = reqwest::Client::builder().build()?;

        // Step 1: Pull peer changes
        let (last_sent_seq, _) = self.store.get_peer_cursor(peer_url)?;
        let changes_url = format!("{}/v1/changes?since={}&limit=500", peer_url, last_sent_seq);
        let resp = client.get(&changes_url).send().await?;
        let batch: SyncBatch = resp.json().await?;

        let new_seq = batch.max_seq;
        let report = self.store.apply_batch(&batch)?;
        self.store.set_peer_cursor(peer_url, new_seq, None)?;

        // Step 2: Push our changes to peer
        let local_batch = self.store.changes_since(0, 500)?;
        let push_url = format!("{}/v1/changes", peer_url);
        let _ = client.post(&push_url).json(&local_batch).send().await?;

        // Step 3: Fetch missing blobs for our device's assignments
        let my_assignments = self.store.list_assignments()?;
        for assign in my_assignments {
            let blob_path = self.store.blob_path(&assign.image_hash);
            if !blob_path.exists() {
                let _ = net::download_blob_resumable(
                    &client,
                    peer_url,
                    &assign.image_hash,
                    None,
                    &blob_path,
                ).await;
            }
        }

        Ok(report)
    }

    pub fn source_manager(&self) -> SourceManager {
        SourceManager::new(self.store.clone())
    }

    pub async fn fetch_unsplash(&self, config: &UnsplashConfig) -> Result<Vec<ImageRecord>> {
        let mgr = self.source_manager();
        let records = mgr.fetch_unsplash(config).await?;
        Ok(records)
    }

    pub async fn fetch_wallhaven(&self, query: Option<&str>, count: usize) -> Result<Vec<ImageRecord>> {
        let mgr = self.source_manager();
        let records = mgr.fetch_wallhaven(query, count).await?;
        Ok(records)
    }

    pub async fn fetch_bing_daily(&self) -> Result<ImageRecord> {
        let mgr = self.source_manager();
        let record = mgr.fetch_bing_daily().await?;
        Ok(record)
    }
}
```

```rust
use std::cmp::Ordering;
use std::fmt;
use std::str::FromStr;
use serde::{Deserialize, Deserializer, Serialize, Serializer};

pub const PIPELINE_VERSION: u32 = 1;

/// BLAKE3 32-byte cryptographic hash with Hex serialization
#[derive(Clone, Copy, PartialEq, Eq, PartialOrd, Ord, Hash)]
pub struct Hash([u8; 32]);

impl Hash {
    pub fn new(bytes: [u8; 32]) -> Self {
        Self(bytes)
    }

    pub fn from_slice(slice: &[u8]) -> Result<Self, String> {
        if slice.len() != 32 {
            return Err("Invalid hash length: expected 32 bytes".to_string());
        }
        let mut arr = [0u8; 32];
        arr.copy_from_slice(slice);
        Ok(Self(arr))
    }

    pub fn compute(data: &[u8]) -> Self {
        let hash = blake3::hash(data);
        Self(*hash.as_bytes())
    }

    pub fn as_bytes(&self) -> &[u8; 32] {
        &self.0
    }

    pub fn to_hex(&self) -> String {
        hex::encode(self.0)
    }
}

impl fmt::Display for Hash {
    fn fmt(&self, f: &mut fmt::Formatter<'_>) -> fmt::Result {
        write!(f, "{}", self.to_hex())
    }
}

impl fmt::Debug for Hash {
    fn fmt(&self, f: &mut fmt::Formatter<'_>) -> fmt::Result {
        write!(f, "Hash({})", self.to_hex())
    }
}

impl FromStr for Hash {
    type Err = String;

    fn from_str(s: &str) -> Result<Self, Self::Err> {
        let bytes = hex::decode(s).map_err(|e| e.to_string())?;
        Self::from_slice(&bytes)
    }
}

impl Serialize for Hash {
    fn serialize<S>(&self, serializer: S) -> Result<S::Ok, S::Error>
    where
        S: Serializer,
    {
        serializer.serialize_str(&self.to_hex())
    }
}

impl<'de> Deserialize<'de> for Hash {
    fn deserialize<D>(deserializer: D) -> Result<Self, D::Error>
    where
        D: Deserializer<'de>,
    {
        let s = String::deserialize(deserializer)?;
        Hash::from_str(&s).map_err(serde::de::Error::custom)
    }
}

/// Hybrid Logical Clock (HLC)
/// Formatted as `<hex_physical_ms>:<hex_counter>:<device_id>`
#[derive(Clone, PartialEq, Eq, Hash)]
pub struct Hlc {
    pub physical_ms: u64,
    pub counter: u32,
    pub device_id: String,
}

impl Hlc {
    pub fn new(physical_ms: u64, counter: u32, device_id: impl Into<String>) -> Self {
        Self {
            physical_ms,
            counter,
            device_id: device_id.into(),
        }
    }

    pub fn zero(device_id: impl Into<String>) -> Self {
        Self {
            physical_ms: 0,
            counter: 0,
            device_id: device_id.into(),
        }
    }

    /// Update HLC on local event
    pub fn tick_local(&mut self, now_ms: u64) {
        if now_ms > self.physical_ms {
            self.physical_ms = now_ms;
            self.counter = 0;
        } else {
            self.counter += 1;
        }
    }

    /// Update HLC when receiving a message from a remote peer.
    /// Rejects or clamps remote clocks > 24 hours ahead of local time to prevent permanent hijacking.
    pub fn tick_remote(&mut self, remote: &Hlc, now_ms: u64) {
        const MAX_SKEW_MS: u64 = 24 * 60 * 60 * 1000;
        let clamped_remote_physical = if remote.physical_ms > now_ms + MAX_SKEW_MS {
            now_ms + MAX_SKEW_MS
        } else {
            remote.physical_ms
        };

        if now_ms > self.physical_ms && now_ms > clamped_remote_physical {
            self.physical_ms = now_ms;
            self.counter = 0;
        } else if self.physical_ms == clamped_remote_physical {
            self.counter = std::cmp::max(self.counter, remote.counter) + 1;
        } else if self.physical_ms > clamped_remote_physical {
            self.counter += 1;
        } else {
            self.physical_ms = clamped_remote_physical;
            self.counter = remote.counter + 1;
        }
    }

    pub fn to_string_repr(&self) -> String {
        format!("{:016x}:{:08x}:{}", self.physical_ms, self.counter, self.device_id)
    }
}

impl fmt::Display for Hlc {
    fn fmt(&self, f: &mut fmt::Formatter<'_>) -> fmt::Result {
        write!(f, "{}", self.to_string_repr())
    }
}

impl fmt::Debug for Hlc {
    fn fmt(&self, f: &mut fmt::Formatter<'_>) -> fmt::Result {
        write!(f, "Hlc({})", self.to_string_repr())
    }
}

impl Ord for Hlc {
    fn cmp(&self, other: &Self) -> Ordering {
        match self.physical_ms.cmp(&other.physical_ms) {
            Ordering::Equal => match self.counter.cmp(&other.counter) {
                Ordering::Equal => self.device_id.cmp(&other.device_id),
                ord => ord,
            },
            ord => ord,
        }
    }
}

impl PartialOrd for Hlc {
    fn partial_cmp(&self, other: &Self) -> Option<Ordering> {
        Some(self.cmp(other))
    }
}

impl FromStr for Hlc {
    type Err = String;

    fn from_str(s: &str) -> Result<Self, Self::Err> {
        let parts: Vec<&str> = s.splitn(3, ':').collect();
        if parts.len() != 3 {
            return Err("Invalid HLC format, expected physical:counter:device_id".to_string());
        }
        let physical_ms = u64::from_str_radix(parts[0], 16).map_err(|e| e.to_string())?;
        let counter = u32::from_str_radix(parts[1], 16).map_err(|e| e.to_string())?;
        let device_id = parts[2].to_string();
        Ok(Self {
            physical_ms,
            counter,
            device_id,
        })
    }
}

impl Serialize for Hlc {
    fn serialize<S>(&self, serializer: S) -> Result<S::Ok, S::Error>
    where
        S: Serializer,
    {
        serializer.serialize_str(&self.to_string_repr())
    }
}

impl<'de> Deserialize<'de> for Hlc {
    fn deserialize<D>(deserializer: D) -> Result<Self, D::Error>
    where
        D: Deserializer<'de>,
    {
        let s = String::deserialize(deserializer)?;
        Hlc::from_str(&s).map_err(serde::de::Error::custom)
    }
}

#[derive(Debug, Clone, Copy, PartialEq, Serialize, Deserialize)]
pub enum Fit {
    Fill,
    Fit,
    CropCenter,
    CropRect { x: f32, y: f32, w: f32, h: f32 },
}

#[derive(Debug, Clone, Copy, PartialEq, Eq, Serialize, Deserialize)]
pub enum OutFormat {
    Png,
    WebpLossy(u8),
}

#[derive(Debug, Clone, PartialEq, Serialize, Deserialize)]
pub struct DerivativeSpec {
    pub target: (u32, u32),
    pub fit: Fit,
    pub focus: Option<(f32, f32)>,
    pub format: OutFormat,
}

impl DerivativeSpec {
    pub fn cache_key(&self, source_hash: &Hash) -> String {
        let canonical_spec = serde_json::to_string(self).unwrap_or_default();
        let mut hasher = blake3::Hasher::new();
        hasher.update(source_hash.as_bytes());
        hasher.update(canonical_spec.as_bytes());
        hasher.update(&PIPELINE_VERSION.to_le_bytes());
        let digest = hasher.finalize();
        let ext = match self.format {
            OutFormat::Png => "png",
            OutFormat::WebpLossy(_) => "webp",
        };
        format!("{}.{}", hex::encode(&digest.as_bytes()[..16]), ext)
    }
}

#[derive(Debug, Clone, PartialEq, Eq, Serialize, Deserialize)]

#[derive(Debug, Clone, Copy, PartialEq, Eq, Serialize, Deserialize)]
pub enum RotationMode {
    Interval(u32), // interval in seconds

#[cfg(test)]
mod tests {
    use super::*;

    #[test]
    fn test_hash_roundtrip() {
        let data = b"sync_wall_wallpaper_bytes";
        let h1 = Hash::compute(data);
        let s = h1.to_string();
        let h2 = Hash::from_str(&s).expect("from_str");
        assert_eq!(h1, h2);
    }

    #[test]
    fn test_hlc_ordering_and_clamping() {
        let mut hlc1 = Hlc::new(1000, 0, "device-a");
        let mut hlc2 = Hlc::new(1000, 0, "device-b");

        // Device ID tie-break
        assert!(hlc1 < hlc2);

        // Local tick advances physical
        hlc1.tick_local(1050);
        assert_eq!(hlc1.physical_ms, 1050);
        assert_eq!(hlc1.counter, 0);

        // Local tick same physical increments counter
        hlc1.tick_local(1050);
        assert_eq!(hlc1.counter, 1);

        // Remote tick clamped if future skewed > 24h
        let extreme_future = Hlc::new(1050 + 48 * 3600 * 1000, 5, "malicious");
        hlc2.tick_remote(&extreme_future, 1050);
        assert!(hlc2.physical_ms <= 1050 + 24 * 3600 * 1000);
    }

    #[test]
    fn test_derivative_spec_cache_key() {
        let h = Hash::compute(b"image_content");
        let spec = DerivativeSpec {
            target: (1920, 1080),
            fit: Fit::Fill,
            focus: Some((0.5, 0.5)),
            format: OutFormat::Png,
        };
        let key1 = spec.cache_key(&h);
        let key2 = spec.cache_key(&h);
        assert_eq!(key1, key2);
        assert!(key1.ends_with(".png"));
    }
}
```

```rust
    Json, Router,

use subtle::ConstantTimeEq;

#[derive(Error, Debug)]
pub enum NetError {
    #[error("TLS/Crypto error: {0}")]
    Crypto(String),
    #[error("Network error: {0}")]
    Reqwest(#[from] reqwest::Error),
    #[error("Store error: {0}")]
    Store(#[from] crate::store::StoreError),
    #[error("IO error: {0}")]
    Io(#[from] std::io::Error),
    #[error("Authentication error: {0}")]
    Auth(String),
    #[error("Not found")]
    NotFound,
}

pub type Result<T> = std::result::Result<T, NetError>;

/// Self-signed TLS certificate and private key with SHA-256 fingerprint
#[derive(Clone)]
pub struct DeviceIdentity {
    pub cert_der: Vec<u8>,
    pub cert_pem: String,
    pub key_pem: String,
    pub fingerprint: String, // SHA-256 hex string (Device ID)
}

impl DeviceIdentity {
    pub fn generate(device_name: &str) -> Result<Self> {
        let key_pair = KeyPair::generate().map_err(|e| NetError::Crypto(e.to_string()))?;
        let mut params = CertificateParams::new(vec![device_name.to_string(), "localhost".to_string()])
            .map_err(|e| NetError::Crypto(e.to_string()))?;
        params.distinguished_name.push(rcgen::DnType::CommonName, device_name);

        let cert = params.self_signed(&key_pair).map_err(|e| NetError::Crypto(e.to_string()))?;
        let cert_der = cert.der().to_vec();
        let cert_pem = cert.pem();
        let key_pem = key_pair.serialize_pem();

        let digest = blake3::hash(&cert_der);
        let fingerprint = hex::encode(digest.as_bytes());

        Ok(Self {
            cert_der,
            cert_pem,
            key_pem,
            fingerprint,
        })
    }

    pub fn save_to_dir<P: AsRef<Path>>(&self, dir: P) -> Result<()> {
        let dir = dir.as_ref();
        fs::create_dir_all(dir)?;
        fs::write(dir.join("cert.pem"), &self.cert_pem)?;
        fs::write(dir.join("key.pem"), &self.key_pem)?;
        fs::write(dir.join("fingerprint.txt"), &self.fingerprint)?;
        Ok(())
    }

    pub fn load_from_dir<P: AsRef<Path>>(dir: P) -> Result<Option<Self>> {
        let dir = dir.as_ref();
        let cert_path = dir.join("cert.pem");
        let key_path = dir.join("key.pem");
        let fp_path = dir.join("fingerprint.txt");

        if !cert_path.exists() || !key_path.exists() || !fp_path.exists() {
            return Ok(None);
        }

        let cert_pem = fs::read_to_string(cert_path)?;
        let key_pem = fs::read_to_string(key_path)?;
        let fingerprint = fs::read_to_string(fp_path)?.trim().to_string();

        let cert_der = pem::parse(&cert_pem)
            .map_err(|e| NetError::Crypto(e.to_string()))?
            .contents()
            .to_vec();

        Ok(Some(Self {
            cert_der,
            cert_pem,
            key_pem,
            fingerprint,
        }))
    }
}

use rustls::client::danger::{ServerCertVerified, ServerCertVerifier};
use rustls::pki_types::{CertificateDer, ServerName, UnixTime};
use rustls::server::danger::{ClientCertVerified, ClientCertVerifier};
use rustls::{DigitallySignedStruct, SignatureScheme};

#[derive(Clone, Debug)]
pub struct PairingSession {
    pub token: String, // 128-bit hex string (32 characters = 16 bytes entropy)
    pub pin: String,   // 6-digit numeric fallback
    pub created_at: std::time::Instant,
    pub failed_attempts: u32,
}

impl Default for PairingSession {
    fn default() -> Self {
        Self::new()
    }
}

impl PairingSession {
    pub fn new() -> Self {
        use rand::RngCore;
        let mut bytes = [0u8; 16];
        rand::thread_rng().fill_bytes(&mut bytes);
        let token = hex::encode(bytes);

        use rand::Rng;
        let pin_num: u32 = rand::thread_rng().gen_range(100_000..1_000_000);
        let pin = pin_num.to_string();

        Self {
            token,
            pin,
            created_at: std::time::Instant::now(),
            failed_attempts: 0,
        }
    }

    pub fn is_expired(&self) -> bool {
        self.created_at.elapsed().as_secs() > 120
    }
}

pub struct ServerState {
    pub store: Arc<Store>,
    pub crop: Arc<CropEngine>,
    pub identity: DeviceIdentity,
    pub active_pairing_token: Arc<parking_lot::Mutex<Option<PairingSession>>>,
}

}

#[derive(Serialize, Deserialize)]
pub struct PairResponse {
```
